Attached are the NAFTA tariff schedules for: 1) industrial goods; as referred to in the NAFTA Market Access Chapter; 2) textiles; and 3) agriculture.

For industrial goods: 

The current package contains separate tariff phase-out code descriptions for each NAFTA party New breakouts are identified in the tariff package with brief descriptions. A more detailed description of all new breakouts is attached at the end of the industrial goods tariff. schedule.

These three documents are in user-friendly statistical language' for working purposes and will be converted to the legal language: of the tariff schedules of the three NAFTA parties. 

For tariff information on specific products, please contact the

August 27, 1992 

USE OF THE NAFTA SIDE-BY-SIDE 

The following document contains the final positions of the United States, Mexico and Canada resulting from the tariff negotiations in the North 

American Free Trade Agreement (NAFTA), for U.S. HS Chapters 25-49 and 64-99. 

The side-by-side is set out as follows.

The HS number is first set out at the internationally agreed 6 digit level, along with the agreed description. 

Beneath each 6 digit heading are the 8-digit line items for each country, followed by the particular country's description for the item. 

The other columns indicate the following: 

Rate: The base tariff rate from which tariff cuts will begin. GSP: Pos: 

For the United States, the "GSP" column, a "G" denotes that an item was eligible for duty-free treatment under the 

Generalized System of Preferences on July 1, 1991.

The final staging category for an item. 

In certain instances, the Parties have agreed to new tariff breakouts. These are noted in the schedules, with a brief description of the breakout. 

Assuming that the NAFTA enters into force on January 1, 1994, the tariff phase-outs would be according to the following schedules. All percentage cuts are from the NAFTA base tariff rate.

Attached is a diskette providing Canada's NAFTA tariff phasing for Chapters 1-98 (including textiles and clothing). 

Also included in the diskette are the U.S. and Mexican NAFTA phasing codes which incorporate the changes from the line-byline review on August 13 & 14th at the Watergate. I understand, however, that there will still be some changes in Agriculture for both the U.S. and Mexico.

While the Canadian phasing incorporates the new phasing codes worked out between U.S. and Mexico.(i.e., B 1 or B-6 for textiles for Canada and Mexico, Ba for light trucks, Ca for passenger vehicles, Cc or C-10 for footwear, paints and dyes, and ceramics), we have not had a chance to update the Mexican and American phasing using the new codes. That is, the C-10s in footwear for U.S. and Mexico are still showing up as Cs. 

However, Canada's C-10 in footwear has been updated. 

The following is a key to the Canadian phasing codes:

Code Count 2269 1016 U A 1010 1947 86 43 566 Canada
